QUEUE 0609..0612 (2026-09-27, seed wave 7, opencode (me-so-poor), on user order 'verify repo plus seed new tasks'): repo verified green this pass, fifth consecutive, compile.bat exit 0 (58 files, -source 6, javac 1.8.0_292), conformance 471/471 PASS exit 0, smoke PASS exit 0, Java 6 grep zero code-context offenders. The user asked how to make the search for OPEN tasks more efficient, prompted by a Deepseek Harness transcript that paged the ledger linearly from line 209 and was still hunting when it ran out of budget, so efficiency and correctness of the census are the subject of this wave rather than incidental to it. FINDING 1, the efficiency answer, already proven this pass: one rg pipeline returns the complete OPEN set in 82ms with no paging and no line bodies, namely rg -o --no-filename with the capture-only pattern anchored to tolerate a leading double pipe; the ledger is a single table whose note lines run to thousands of characters, so every tool that reads whole lines pays for prose it does not need, and a naive leading-pipe anchor silently misses the very rows the census exists to find. This yields 25 OPEN IDs. FINDING 2, and the reason a regex alone is not the fix, a CORRECTNESS bug rather than a speed one: the same transcript concluded that the opencode (me-so-poor) heartbeat claims B5-0572, B5-0573, B5-0574 and B5-0577, but that heartbeat records live_claims as an empty list and current_task as null, and this seeder holds no claim; those four IDs appear only inside the narrative last_completed prose string. A reader that greps a JSON blob for task IDs manufactures a claim set out of English. Making the query faster over a worse data model would only deliver a wrong answer sooner, so B5-0609 seeds a read-only query tool that owns the join and parses structured fields only, never prose. FINDING 3, THIRD consecutive pass where a cheap liveness signal pointed at live or absent data rather than truth, and this time it was self-inflicted: the heartbeat lookup in this seeder own census used an exact agent_id join, which cannot match because the B5-0572 claim spells the owner solar-pro4:free with an ASCII colon while the heartbeat filename carries U+2028 between pro4 and free, so the lookup matched nothing, returned -1, and -1 compared as YOUNGER than the 30-minute TTL, rendering four claims LIVE with no verification at all; the raw value had to be printed to notice. An absent signal must be UNKNOWN, never live. B5-0597 is amended with both the fail-loud rule and the mandatory punctuation-normalised join, and B5-0598 is amended because the same prose-in-JSON hazard corrupts any cross-reference audit that greps heartbeats. FINDING 4, ID band collision avoided: another agent seeded B5-0604 through B5-0608 between this pass pre-write census and its write, so this wave starts at B5-0609 rather than 0600 and leaves no gap. FINDING 5, the pipe-hygiene backlog is GROWING, not draining, which is the opposite of the steady state the repair rows assume: all five of those fresh rows 0604, 0605, 0606, 0607 and 0608 were seeded with a leading double pipe at 8 pipes each, so a single seeding pass added five defective rows while the existing repair tasks still hold eleven, meaning the class is being produced faster than it is being fixed. B5-0611 owns that re-sweep, gated behind B5-0607. FINDING 6, the substantive non-governance seed: the card resources carry 383 cards in deluxe and 446 in premiere with zero ID overlap and no duplicate IDs within deluxe, across 7 faction values and 9 types, and the NON_ALIGNED faction holds only 4 cards in each set against 25 to 41 for every other playable race, while the rulebook names the League of Non-Aligned Worlds as one of five playable races, so B5-0610 must first determine whether playable-race identity is encoded in faction or in subtype, because the answer decides whether LoaW is a supported faction or an unplayable one, and must also rule on the Status Tracker card, which the rulebook documents in section IV but which appears as no card type in either file. QUEUE 0613..0614 (2026-09-27, seed wave 8, opencode (me-so-poor), on user order 'is there any way to implement this fix for all models'): the user asked how to make the census fix apply to EVERY agent using the repo, not just this one, and the answer turned out to be that a repo-wide fix already exists and is broken. `.agent/run-queue.ps1` (7707 bytes, the queue runner every model is expected to drive) was located and audited, and it carries the exact defect this seeder has been hand-rolling around for four passes. Its Get-LedgerRows filters rows with the single-leading-pipe regex '^\|\s*(B5-\d+)\s*\|' and splits whole lines, so it cannot see 14 of the 312 current rows, being 298 seen against 312 existing, and 8 of those 14 are OPEN: B5-0572, B5-0574, B5-0604, B5-0605, B5-0606, B5-0607 and B5-0608, plus DONE rows 0202c, 0330a, 0331a, 0329a, 0568, 0571 and 0573. Consequence, and it is the serious one: the runner's stop condition (a) is 'no OPEN task without a live claim remains, therefore queue drained', so with 8 OPEN rows invisible it would report the queue DRAINED while at minimum 6 unclaimed claimable tasks sit in the ledger, because 0572 and 0574 hold live claims. The malformed rows are self-hiding from the automation that exists to find them, which is why this class kept growing across five passes while the backlog counter looked healthy. Two further defects in the same tool: Test-LiveClaim derives liveness from the claim file's own started_utc and ignores the owning agent's heartbeat and any report, which is precisely the false-stale signal that made claims B5-0573 and B5-0574 look reaping-eligible at 45 minutes while their owner was demonstrably alive, and it is worth noting the tool already fails safe by returning true on an unreadable claim, so the fix is to extend that same caution to the missing-signal case rather than to loosen it. Third, the prereq and lane tables are hardcoded to the 0367-0389 era and contain none of the 29 current OPEN rows, so every gate stated in current row text, including the many gated-on-DONE chains seeded across waves 3 to 8, is invisible to the runner and it would offer gated work as free. Fourth, and the reason the fix does not reach other models today even if the tool were perfect: `00_BOOT.md` contains no reference to run-queue.ps1 at all, verified by search, so a model following the boot protocol has no way to learn the tool exists and will hand-roll a census exactly as this seeder and the Deepseek Harness both did. So the repo-wide fix is not a new tool, it is B5-0613, which repairs the runner every model already shares and adds the one boot line that makes it discoverable. B5-0609 from the previous wave stays as the richer reporting census, and the two should share a row parser rather than diverge into a second implementation. Also recorded as a root-cause datum for whoever sizes the work: the ledger is 483008 bytes over 730 lines, averaging 662 characters per line, with 236 lines exceeding 1000 characters accounting for 72.6 percent of all bytes, so the file is genuinely too large to page and query tooling is mitigation rather than cure. Repo re-verified green this pass, sixth consecutive, compile.bat exit 0, conformance 471/471 PASS exit 0, smoke PASS exit 0. Nothing seeded on the 0454 brief with mercenary and contingency staying data-gated per Ruling 2c and 3c. Code-task gate unchanged. Close-outs touch their ledger row only and preserve table pipes exactly per 00_BOOT step 8, seed every new row with a single leading pipe, never write a pipe character in note text per the 0435 standing rule, and never write-file a shared governance file to append.

TWO SEEDS ONLY, and the restraint is itself the finding.

Census re-read immediately before writing, per the record filed four passes ago, and it earned its keep twice over. Rows B5-0613 and B5-0614 appeared at the tail WHILE this pass was verifying, seeded by a concurrent writer, so the pre-write grep is the only reason this pass did not collide on those two IDs. That is the live mirror of the collision class B5-0344 recorded, and it is direct proof that a census taken without a final re-read manufactures duplicate IDs.

FINDING 1, the urgent seed, a FALSE GATE sitting in the ledger right now. Row B5-0606 opens with the words 'gate satisfied' and cites its grounding as report .agent/REPORTS/2026-09-26-opencode (me-so-poor)-B5-0594.md. Verified this pass: that report file does not exist on disk, and row B5-0594 is still OPEN, not DONE. So the only substantive code task in the tail band, being the rulebook-coverage gap closure slice, is gated on an audit that was never delivered, while asserting authority it does not have. This is what AGENTS.md section 3 forbids, namely authority asserted from narrative rather than from artifact. It matters more than a typical ledger defect because B5-0606 is one of only two OPEN rows in the entire queue that touch b5ccg/src, so an agent trusting it would implement against a nonexistent audit and report conformance coverage that was never established. B5-0594 remains OPEN and unclaimed and is the real prerequisite.

FINDING 2, an independent correction to the B5-0613 seed note, measured rather than accepted. The note claims the shared runner .agent/run-queue.ps1 sees 298 of 312 rows and that 8 of the 14 missed rows are OPEN. Measured this pass: Get-LedgerRows requires the ID to appear immediately after a single leading pipe, so the runner sees 300 rows, misses 14, and exactly 7 of the missed are OPEN, being B5-0572, B5-0574, B5-0604, B5-0605, B5-0606, B5-0607 and B5-0608. The note lists those 7 IDs while claiming a count of 8, so its ID list was right and its arithmetic was not. B5-0613 remains the highest-value row in the queue and this correction does not weaken it; it is recorded so the 0613 owner repairs the right number. Root cause is now independently confirmed rather than asserted: a single-leading-pipe anchor plus a split that reads cell 1, which is the empty ID cell on precisely the rows the anchor fails to match.

FINDING 3, and this is why the wave seeds two rows and not five. Composition of the 30 OPEN rows: 8 are gated checkpoint commits, 4 are pipe and delimiter repairs, 3 are proposals about proposals, 5 are read-only audits, 1 is a frontmatter compaction, 1 is a read-only query tool, and exactly 2 touch b5ccg/src. Six consecutive seed waves have added zero game features while adding roughly forty governance rows. The mechanism is now identified rather than mysterious: agents hand-roll a census because the shared runner is broken, which is B5-0613, and undocumented in the boot protocol, which is B5-0614, so each wave spends its budget re-deriving queue state and files the re-derivation as a new task. Repairing the tool and documenting it is worth more than any further governance seed, which is why this wave files none of that class. B5-0589, the stall-soak probe, is already seeded and is the real upstream enabler for the live-claimed B5-0577, so no duplicate was filed for it.

Liveness note, no reap attempted. Claims B5-0572, B5-0573 and B5-0574 read 63 and 85 minutes against a 30-minute TTL and look reaping-eligible, but their owner was writing reports in the same window, which is exactly the false-reap hazard B5-0597 exists to prevent, so they were left in place per that filed lesson. B5-0577's claim was released during this pass and its row remains OPEN. Claims B5-0583 and B5-0584 were live at 2 and 9 minutes and were untouched.

Nothing seeded on the 0454 brief. Ruling 2c and 3c are a standing human directive that no further mercenary or contingency seeds be filed until a source set is approved, so the apparent parked-feature backlog is a decision and not a stall, and this wave does not relitigate it. The B5-0497 enhancement slices were checked and are already closed by B5-0506 and B5-0528, and the rulebook section IV assistant mechanic is already implemented by B5-0401 with 138 references across src, so no seed was filed on either.

The liveness picture is recorded precisely because the filed B5-0597 lesson demands it, and it is NOT the expected one: the owner is ALIVE, heartbeat `solar-pro4<U+2028>free.json` at `02:53:04Z`, thirteen minutes before the reap, so this is emphatically NOT a stalled-agent reap. Deletion is therefore safe by a much stronger argument than staleness: a DONE row can never be offered by the runner and can never be claimed, so removing these two files cannot hand a live task to a second claimant, which is the specific harm the B5-0597 lesson was written to prevent. This also exposes a defect class that is the mirror image of the B5-0618 ID collision and does not appear to be logged anywhere yet: a claim file can be created for a task that another agent has already closed, because the claim protocol at 00_BOOT step 6 and in `CLAIMS/README.md` checks only that the claim FILE is absent and never that the ledger ROW is still OPEN. Here the claimant was not stale and not careless about liveness; the check that would have caught it simply does not exist in the protocol.
